{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m}}(t,k)-\widehat{X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m}}(s,k)\big|^2\lesssim|t-s|^{2\zeta+\vartheta}\varepsilon^{-4\zeta-2\vartheta}\sum_{k_1+\cdots+k_m=k}\prod_{i=1}^m\frac{|f(\varepsilon k_i)|^2}{1+|k_i|^2}.
\end{align*}
Set $K:=2^q\vee1$ and
\begin{align*}
S_{m,\varepsilon}(K):=\sum_{|k|\sim K}\sum_{k_1+\cdots+k_m=k}\prod_{i=1}^m\frac{|f(\varepsilon k_i)|^2}{1+|k_i|^2}.
\end{align*}
We claim that, for every $\vartheta>0$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:dyadic-conv-renorm}
S_{m,\varepsilon}(K)\lesssim_{\vartheta} K^{m}+{\boldsymbol{1}}_{ \{m\ge3\} }K^{3}\varepsilon^{3-m-\vartheta}.
\end{align}

To prove \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-renorm}, decompose the sum according to dyadic $N$ such that $N\sim 1+{\mathord{{\rm max}}}_i|k_i|$. If $N\lesssim K$, then
\begin{align*}
S_{m,\varepsilon}(K)\lesssim\prod_{i=1}^m\sum_{|k_i|\lesssim K}\frac1{1+|k_i|^2}\lesssim K^m.
\end{align*}
If $N\gg K$, the condition $|k|\sim K$ implies that at least two input frequencies have size comparable to $N$. Assume, without loss of generality, that $|k_1|\sim|k_2|\sim N$. For fixed $k,k_3,\ldots,k_m$, 
\begin{align*}
\sum_{\substack{k_1+k_2=k-k_3-\cdots-k_m\\|k_1|\sim|k_2|\sim N}}\frac1{(1+|k_1|^2)(1+|k_2|^2)}\lesssim N^{-1}.
\end{align*}
Therefore, the contribution of this scale is bounded by
\begin{align*}
K^3N^{-1}\prod_{i=3}^m\sum_{|k_i|\lesssim N}\frac1{1+|k_i|^2}\lesssim K^3N^{m-3}.
\end{align*}
Summing over $K\ll N\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}$ gives
\begin{align*}
\sum_{K\ll N\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}K^3N^{m-3}
\lesssim
\begin{cases}
K^2, & m=2,\\
K^3\log(2+\varepsilon^{-1}K^{-1}), & m=3,\\
K^3\varepsilon^{3-m}, & m=4,5.
\end{cases}
\end{align*}
The first term is absorbed by $K^m$, while $\log(2+\varepsilon^{-1}K^{-1})\lesssim_{\vartheta}\varepsilon^{-\vartheta}$. This proves \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-renorm}.

We next derive the $L^\infty$ block estimate. Fix $\vartheta>0$ and $r,\bar p<\infty$ such that
$$2\zeta+\vartheta<1,\ 2\vartheta\leqslant\eta,\ \frac3r\leqslant\vartheta,\ \bar p\geqslant p\vee r,\ \frac{\bar p\vartheta}{2}>1.$$
By the Bernstein inequality, the Minkowski inequality and hypercontractivity on the $m$-th homogeneous Wiener chaos,
\begin{align*}
\left\|\Delta_q\big(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m}(t)-X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m}(s)\big)\right\|_{L^{\bar p}(\Omega;L^\infty)}&\lesssim K^{3/r}\left\|\Delta_q\big(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m}(t)-X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m}(s)\big)\right\|_{L^{\bar p}(\Omega;L^r)}\\
&\lesssim K^\vartheta\sup_{x\in{\mathbb T}^3}\Big({\mathbb E}\big|\Delta_q\big(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m}(t)-X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m}(s)\big)(x)\big|^2\Big)^{\frac12}.
\end{align*}
By \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-renorm},
\begin{align*}
\left\|\Delta_q\big(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m}(t)-X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m}(s)\big)\right\|_{L^{\bar p}(\Omega;L^\infty)}\lesssim|t-s|^{\zeta+\frac\vartheta2}\varepsilon^{-2\zeta-\eta}\big(K^{\frac m2+\eta}+{\boldsymbol{1}}_{\{m\geqslant3\}}\varepsilon^{\frac{3-m}{2}}K^{\frac32+\eta}\big).
\end{align*}
The corresponding fixed-time estimate follows in the same way and is bounded by the same right-hand side without the time factor. Since $\frac{\bar p\vartheta}2>1$, the Banach-space-valued Garsia--Rodemich--Rumsey lemma proves \eqref{wick-dyadic-01}.

We next consider the integrated object. For fixed $k$, set $A:=\sum_{i=1}^m a_{k_i}$. A direct calculation gives
\begin{align*}
\int_{-\infty}^t\int_{-\infty}^t\mathrm{e}^{-a_k(t-r)}\mathrm{e}^{-a_k(t-r')}\mathrm{e}^{-A|r-r'|}{\mathord{{\rm d}}} r{\mathord{{\rm d}}} r'=\frac1{a_k(a_k+A)}.
\end{align*}
Consequently, for $|k|\sim K$,
\begin{align*}
{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m})}(t,k)\big|^2\lesssim\sum_{k_1+\cdots+k_m=k}\prod_{i=1}^m\frac{|f(\varepsilon k_i)|^2}{1+|k_i|^2}\frac1{(1+K^2)(1+K^2+\sum_i|k_i|^2)}.
\end{align*}
Define
\begin{align*}
\widetilde S_{m,\varepsilon}(K):=\sum_{|k|\sim K}\sum_{k_1+\cdots+k_m=k}\prod_{i=1}^m\frac{|f(\varepsilon k_i)|^2}{1+|k_i|^2}\frac1{(1+K^2)(1+K^2+\sum_i|k_i|^2)}.
\end{align*}
We claim that, for every $\vartheta>0$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:dyadic-conv-integrated-renorm}
\widetilde S_{m,\varepsilon}(K)\lesssim_{\vartheta}\varepsilon^{-\vartheta}K^{m-4},\ m=2,\ldots,5.
\end{align}
Indeed, if $N\lesssim K$, the denominator in the definition of $\widetilde S_{m,\varepsilon}(K)$ is bounded below by a constant multiple of $K^4$, and the preceding low-frequency estimate gives $K^{m-4}$. If $N\gg K$, the two-large-frequency estimate above gives $K^3N^{m-3}$, while the denominator is bounded below by a constant multiple of $K^2N^2$. Consequently,
\begin{align*}
\widetilde S_{m,\varepsilon}(K)\lesssim K^{m-4}+K\sum_{K\ll N\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}N^{m-5}\lesssim_{\vartheta}\varepsilon^{-\vartheta}K^{m-4}.
\end{align*}
For $m=5$, the last sum produces $K\log(2+\varepsilon^{-1}K^{-1})$, which is absorbed into $\varepsilon^{-\vartheta}K$. This proves \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-integrated-renorm}.

For the time increment of ${\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m})$, write $h=t-s>0$ and split
\begin{align*}
\widehat{{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m})}(t,k)-\widehat{{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m})}(s,k)
=(\mathrm{e}^{-a_kh}-1)\widehat{{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m})}(s,k)+\int_s^t\mathrm{e}^{-a_k(t-r)}\widehat{X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m}}(r,k){\mathord{{\rm d}}} r.
\end{align*}
The first term is controlled by the fixed-time estimate and the bound
\begin{align*}
|1-\mathrm{e}^{-a_kh}|\lesssim h^{\zeta+\frac\vartheta2}a_k^{\zeta+\frac\vartheta2}\lesssim h^{\zeta+\frac\vartheta2}K^{2\zeta+\vartheta}.
\end{align*}
For the second term we use the following elementary kernel estimate. Let $a\ge1$, $A>0$, and $\gamma:=2\zeta+\vartheta\in(0,1)$. Then
\begin{align}
\int_0^h\int_0^h \mathrm{e}^{-a(u+v)}\mathrm{e}^{-A|u-v|}{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u{\mathord{{\rm d}}} v\lesssim \frac{a^\gamma h^\gamma}{a(a+A)}.\nonumber
\end{align}
Applying this estimate with $a=a_k$ and $A=\sum_i a_{k_i}$ gives
\begin{align*}
\sup_x{\mathbb E}\left|\Delta_q\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m})(t)-{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m})(s)\big)(x)\right|^2\lesssim |t-s|^{2\zeta+\vartheta}K^{4\zeta+2\vartheta}\widetilde S_{m,\varepsilon}(K).
\end{align*}
Combining this bound with \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-integrated-renorm}, and using the same Bernstein and hypercontractivity argument as above, yields
\begin{align*}
\left\|\Delta_q\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m})(t)-{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m})(s)\big)\right\|_{L^{\bar p}(\Omega;L^\infty)}\lesssim |t-s|^{\zeta+\frac\vartheta2}\varepsilon^{-\eta}K^{\frac m2-2+2\zeta+\eta}.
\end{align*}
The fixed-time estimate is identical without the factor $|t-s|^{\zeta+\frac\vartheta2}$. Another application of the Banach-space-valued Garsia--Rodemich--Rumsey lemma proves \eqref{wick-dyadic-02}.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- proposition-8: body_md -->
\label{prop:eps-wick-vanishing}
Let $T>0$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, and let $\delta>0$ and $\delta'\in(0,\frac\alpha2-\frac14)$ be sufficiently small. Then, for $m=2,3,4$,
\begin{align*}
\varepsilon^\alpha X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m}\to0\ \text{in }L^p\big(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,\alpha-\frac m2-\delta-2\delta'}\big).
\end{align*}
Moreover, for $m=2,3,4,5$,
\begin{align*}
\varepsilon^\alpha {\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m})\to0\ \text{in }L^p\big(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,\alpha+2-\frac m2-\delta-2\delta'}\big).
\end{align*}
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
Choose $\eta>0$ so small that $\eta<\frac\delta2$ and $2\delta'+\eta<\alpha-\frac12$. Set $K:=2^q\vee1$. By the Fourier cutoff, it suffices to consider
$1\leqslant K\leqslant R_f\varepsilon^{-1}$. Moreover,
$$\|F_\varepsilon\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\rho})}\lesssim\Big(\sum_{2^q\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{p\rho}\|\Delta_qF_\varepsilon\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_t^{\delta'}L^\infty)}^p\Big)^{1/p},$$
where $F_\varepsilon$ denotes any of the random fields considered below. Consequently, a weighted dyadic bound of the form $A_\varepsilon K^\beta$ contributes at most $A_\varepsilon\varepsilon^{-\beta_+}$ after geometric summation.

For the first claim, take $\zeta=\delta'$ in \eqref{wick-dyadic-01}. For $m=2,3,4$,
\begin{align*}
&\big\|\varepsilon^\alpha X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m}\big\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\alpha-\frac m2-\delta-2\delta'})}\\
&\qquad \lesssim\varepsilon^{\alpha-2\delta'-\eta}\sup_{1\le K\le R_f\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{\alpha-\delta-2\delta'+\eta}+{\boldsymbol{1}}_{\{m\ge3\}}\varepsilon^{\alpha-2\delta'-\eta+\frac{3-m}2}\sup_{1\le K\le R_f\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{\alpha+\frac32-\frac m2-\delta-2\delta'+\eta}\\
&\qquad \lesssim\varepsilon^{\alpha-2\delta'-\eta-(\alpha-\delta-2\delta'+\eta)_+}+{\boldsymbol{1}}_{\{m\ge3\}}\varepsilon^{\alpha-2\delta'-\eta+\frac{3-m}2-(\alpha+\frac32-\frac m2-\delta-2\delta'+\eta)_+}.
\end{align*}
The two exponents on the right-hand side are bounded from below, respectively, by ${\mathord{{\rm min}}}\{\alpha-2\delta'-\eta,\delta-2\eta\}$ and ${\mathord{{\rm min}}}\left\{\alpha-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta,\delta-2\eta\right\}$, which are positive by the choice of $\eta$. This proves the first convergence.

For the integrated terms, take $\zeta=\delta'$ in
\eqref{wick-dyadic-02}. For $m=2,\ldots,5$,
\begin{align*}
\big\|\varepsilon^\alpha {\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond m})\big\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\alpha+2-\frac m2-\delta-2\delta'})}&\lesssim\varepsilon^{\alpha-\eta}\sup_{1\le K\le R_f\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{\alpha+2-\frac m2-\delta-2\delta'}K^{\frac m2-2+2\delta'+\eta}\\
&=\varepsilon^{\alpha-\eta}\sup_{1\le K\le R_f\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{\alpha-\delta+\eta}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\alpha-\eta-(\alpha-\delta+\eta)_+}.
\end{align*}
The last exponent is bounded from below by ${\mathord{{\rm min}}}\{\alpha-\eta,\delta-2\eta\}>0$, which proves the second convergence.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
\label{rem:m5-not-standalone}
The non-integrated estimate is not stated for $m=5$, and this omission is essential. Indeed, for every fixed $t$, ${\mathbb E}|\widehat{\varepsilon^\alpha X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5}}(t,0)|^2\asymp \varepsilon^{2\alpha-2}$, which diverges as $\varepsilon\to0$ since $\alpha<1$. Thus, $\varepsilon^\alpha X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5}$ does not vanish even at the level of its zero spatial Fourier mode. What is needed in the mild formulation is instead the integrated estimate $\varepsilon^\alpha{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\longrightarrow0$, which is precisely the second assertion of Proposition~\ref{prop:eps-wick-vanishing}.
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- lemma-15: body_md -->
\label{lem:X5-X3-vanishing}
Let $T>0$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\delta,\delta'>0$ sufficiently small such that $\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'>0$. Then
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X3-vanishing}
\varepsilon^{2\alpha}\,{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}\to0\ \text{in}\ L^p\big(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,2\alpha-2-\delta-2\delta'}\big).
\end{align}
More precisely, if $K:=2^q\vee1$, then, for every sufficiently small $\eta>0$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X3-dyadic}
\left\|\Delta_q\left(\varepsilon^{2\alpha}\,{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}\right)\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C^{\delta'}([0,T];L^\infty))}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{2\alpha-1-2\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta}.
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-15 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
By the Wick formula,
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X3-wick-expansion}
{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}=\sum_{r=0}^3c_r{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r},\ c_r:=\binom5r\binom3r r!,
\end{align}
where ${\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}$ denotes the contribution arising from exactly $r$ cross-contractions between $X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5}$ and $X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}$. More explicitly,
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X3-Tr-Fourier}
\widehat{{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}}(t,n)={}\sum_{k+\ell=n}\Phi(k,\ell)\int_0^\infty\mathrm{e}^{-a_ku}\sum_{\substack{k_1+\cdots+k_5=k\\\ell_1+\ell_2+\ell_3=\ell}}\prod_{\nu=1}^r\left({\boldsymbol{1}}_{\{k_\nu+\ell_\nu=0\}}w_\varepsilon(k_\nu)\mathrm{e}^{-a_{k_\nu}u}\right)&\\
\times:\prod_{\nu=r+1}^5\widehat X_\varepsilon(t-u,k_\nu)\prod_{\mu=r+1}^3\widehat X_\varepsilon(t,\ell_\mu):\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u&.\notag
\end{align}
The term ${\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}$ belongs to the homogeneous Wiener chaos of order $8-2r$. Since $f$ is compactly supported, there is a constant $R_f<\infty$ such that all the blocks in \eqref{eq:X5-X3-wick-expansion} vanish whenever $K>R_f\varepsilon^{-1}$.

We claim that, for every $\vartheta>0$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X3-r012-second-moment}
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\left|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}}(t,n)\right|^2\lesssim_\vartheta\varepsilon^{-1-\vartheta}K^{3+\vartheta},\ r=0,1,2.
\end{align}
To prove this, we first record the convolution estimates used below. The same dyadic argument as in the proof of \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-renorm} gives, for every $\vartheta>0$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X3-basic-convolutions}
G_{2,\varepsilon}(b)\lesssim\varrho(b)^{-1},\ G_{3,\varepsilon}(b)\lesssim_\vartheta\Lambda^\vartheta,\ G_{4,\varepsilon}(b)\lesssim_\vartheta\Lambda^{1+\vartheta}.
\end{align}
where the logarithmic borderline in the case $m=3$ is absorbed into $\Lambda^\vartheta$. Likewise, summing \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-integrated-renorm} for $m=5$ over the dyadic  blocks yields
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X3-integrated-five-sum}
\sum_{b\in{\mathbb Z}^3}{\mathcal J}_{5,\varepsilon}(b)\lesssim_\vartheta\Lambda^{1+\vartheta}.
\end{align}

Set $m_1:=5-r$, $m_2:=3-r$, and write $\mathbf p=(p_1,\ldots,p_{m_1})$, $\mathbf z=(z_1,\ldots,z_{m_2})$ with $P:=\sum_i p_i$ and $Q:=\sum_jz_j$. By the Wiener isometry applied to the ordered kernel, together with the contractivity of symmetrization and the evenness of $a_\xi$ and $w_\varepsilon(\xi)$, for each fixed $n$ we obtain,
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim{\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,r}(n),$$
where
$${\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,r}(n):={}\sum_{\substack{\mathbf p\in({\mathbb Z}^3)^{m_1},\mathbf z\in({\mathbb Z}^3)^{m_2}\\P+Q=n}}\prod_{i=1}^{m_1}w_\varepsilon(p_i)\prod_{j=1}^{m_2}w_\varepsilon(z_j)\times\int_0^\infty\int_0^\infty\mathrm{e}^{-A_{\mathbf p}|u-v|}H_{\varepsilon,r}(P,u)H_{\varepsilon,r}(P,v)\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} v.$$

For $r=0$,
\begin{align*}
\int_0^\infty\int_0^\infty\mathrm{e}^{-a_P(u+v)}\mathrm{e}^{-A_{\mathbf p}|u-v|}\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} v=\frac1{a_P(a_P+A_{\mathbf p})}.
\end{align*}
For $r=1,2$, the inequality
\begin{align*}
a_{P+\Xi}+\sum_{\nu=1}^ra_{\xi_\nu}\gtrsim a_P+\sum_{\nu=1}^ra_{\xi_\nu}
\end{align*}
implies
\begin{align*}
H_{\varepsilon,r}(P,u)\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-ca_Pu}\Theta_\varepsilon(u)^r,\qquad\Theta_\varepsilon(u):=\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb Z}^3}w_\varepsilon(\xi)\mathrm{e}^{-ca_\xi u}\lesssim\Lambda\wedge u^{-\frac12}.
\end{align*}
Consequently,
\begin{align*}
\int_0^\infty H_{\varepsilon,1}(P,u)\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u\lesssim a_P^{-\frac12},\ \int_0^\infty H_{\varepsilon,2}(P,u)\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u\lesssim\log(2+\Lambda).
\end{align*}
Using \eqref{eq:X5-X3-basic-convolutions} and \eqref{eq:X5-X3-integrated-five-sum}, we obtain, after absorbing logarithmic losses into $\Lambda^\vartheta$,
$${\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,0}(n)\lesssim\sup_{b\in{\mathbb Z}^3}G_{3,\varepsilon}(b)\sum_{P\in{\mathbb Z}^3}{\mathcal J}_{5,\varepsilon}(P)\lesssim_\vartheta\Lambda^{1+\vartheta},$$
$${\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,1}(n)\lesssim\sum_{P\in{\mathbb Z}^3}a_P^{-1}G_{4,\varepsilon}(P)G_{2,\varepsilon}(n-P)\lesssim_\vartheta\Lambda^{1+\vartheta}\sum_{\varrho(P)\lesssim\Lambda}\varrho(P)^{-2}\varrho(n-P)^{-1}\lesssim_\vartheta\Lambda^{1+\vartheta},$$
$${\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,2}(n)\lesssim\log^2(2+\Lambda)\sum_{P\in{\mathbb Z}^3}G_{3,\varepsilon}(P)G_{1,\varepsilon}(n-P)=\log^2(2+\Lambda)G_{4,\varepsilon}(n)\lesssim_\vartheta\Lambda^{1+\vartheta}.$$
Since $\Lambda\asymp\varepsilon^{-1}$ and the block $|n|\sim K$ contains $O(K^3)$ frequencies, this proves \eqref{eq:X5-X3-r012-second-moment}.

We next consider $r=3$. All three factors in $X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}$ are then contracted, leaving a second-chaos term. Writing $\xi_{1,2,3}:=\xi_1+\xi_2+\xi_3$, define
$$J_{\varepsilon,n}(u):={}\sum_{\xi_1,\xi_2,\xi_3\in{\mathbb Z}^3}\Phi(n+\xi_{1,2,3},-\xi_{1,2,3})\prod_{\nu=1}^3w_\varepsilon(\xi_\nu)\times\mathrm{e}^{-\left(a_{n+\xi_{1,2,3}}+a_{\xi_1}+a_{\xi_2}+a_{\xi_3}\right)u}.$$
Then
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X3-r3-kernel-representation}
\widehat{{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,3}}(t,n)=\sum_{p_1+p_2=n}\int_0^\infty J_{\varepsilon,n}(u):\widehat X_\varepsilon(t-u,p_1)\widehat X_\varepsilon(t-u,p_2):\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u.
\end{align}
Since $|\Phi|\lesssim1$ and $a_{n+\xi_{1,2,3}}+a_{\xi_1}+a_{\xi_2}+a_{\xi_3}\gtrsim1+|\xi_1|^2+|\xi_2|^2+|\xi_3|^2$, a dyadic decomposition gives
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X3-r3-kernel-L1}
\sup_{n\in{\mathbb Z}^3}\int_0^\infty|J_{\varepsilon,n}(u)|\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u\lesssim\sum_{\xi_1,\xi_2,\xi_3}\frac{\prod_{\nu=1}^3|f(\varepsilon\xi_\nu)|^2}{\prod_{\nu=1}^3(1+|\xi_\nu|^2)\left(1+|\xi_1|^2+|\xi_2|^2+|\xi_3|^2\right)}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}.
\end{align}
Indeed, on a dyadic scale $N\sim{\mathord{{\rm max}}}_\nu\varrho(\xi_\nu)$, the contribution is bounded by
\begin{align*}
N^{-2}\prod_{\nu=1}^3\sum_{\varrho(\xi_\nu)\lesssim N}\varrho(\xi_\nu)^{-2}\lesssim N.
\end{align*}
Summing over $N\lesssim\Lambda$ proves \eqref{eq:X5-X3-r3-kernel-L1}.

Since ${\mathbb E}|\widehat{X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2}}(t,n)|^2\lesssim G_{2,\varepsilon}(n)$, the Minkowski inequality and \eqref{eq:X5-X3-r3-kernel-L1} give
\begin{align*}
{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,3}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2}\varrho(n)^{-1}.
\end{align*}
Therefore,
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X3-r3-second-moment}
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,3}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2}K^2.
\end{align}

We next estimate the time increments. Fix $\vartheta>0$ sufficiently small that $2\delta'+\vartheta<1$, and let $h:=t-s>0$. Then
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X3-first-chaos-increment}
{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat X_\varepsilon(t,k)-\widehat X_\varepsilon(s,k)\big|^2=2w_\varepsilon(k)(1-\mathrm{e}^{-a_kh})\lesssim h^{2\delta'+\vartheta}\varepsilon^{-4\delta'-2\vartheta}w_\varepsilon(k).
\end{align}
Since the heat kernels above depend only on $u$, replacing $t$ by $s$ changes only the factors involving $X_\varepsilon$. Expanding the difference of the products, each resulting term contains one increment of $X_\varepsilon$, while all remaining factors are estimated as in the fixed-time case. The Wiener isometry and the Cauchy--Schwarz inequality then allow us to apply \eqref{eq:X5-X3-first-chaos-increment} to this increment.

For $r=0$, each increment contributes the factor $h^{2\delta'+\vartheta}\varepsilon^{-4\delta'-2\vartheta}$. When the increment occurs in a factor inside ${\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})$ with frequency $p_i$, the identity $P=\sum_jp_j$ gives $a_{p_i}\lesssim a_P+\sum_{j\ne i}a_{p_j}$, so the same time-integral estimate remains valid. For $r=1,2$, no additional argument is needed, since the fixed-time estimate already used $\mathrm{e}^{-A_{\mathbf p}|u-v|}\leqslant1$. Hence
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X3-r012-time-increment}
\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\big({\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}(t)-{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}(s)\big)}(n)\big|^2\lesssim_\vartheta h^{2\delta'+\vartheta}\varepsilon^{-1-4\delta'-3\vartheta}K^{3+\vartheta},\ r=0,1,2.
\end{align}

For $r=3$, the kernel $J_{\varepsilon,n}(u)$ is independent of $t$. Moreover,
\begin{align*}
{\mathbb E}\left|\widehat{X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2}}(t,n)-\widehat{X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2}}(s,n)\right|^2\lesssim h^{2\delta'+\vartheta}\varepsilon^{-4\delta'-2\vartheta}G_{2,\varepsilon}(n).
\end{align*}
Thus, by \eqref{eq:X5-X3-r3-kernel-representation} and \eqref{eq:X5-X3-r3-kernel-L1},
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X3-r3-time-increment}
\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\left|\widehat{\Delta_q\big({\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,3}(t)-{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,3}(s)\big)}(n)\right|^2\lesssim h^{2\delta'+\vartheta}\varepsilon^{-2-4\delta'-2\vartheta}K^2.
\end{align}

Combining the preceding fixed-time and increment bounds with the Bernstein inequality, hypercontractivity, and the Banach-space-valued Garsia--Rodemich--Rumsey lemma, as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:wick-dyadic-vanishing}, we obtain, after absorbing all auxiliary losses into $\eta$,
\begin{align*}
\left\|\Delta_q{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C^{\delta'}([0,T];L^\infty))}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}
\begin{cases}
\varepsilon^{-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}K^{\frac32+\eta},& r=0,1,2,\\[2mm]
\varepsilon^{-1-2\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta},& r=3.
\end{cases}
\end{align*}
All blocks vanish unless $K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}$. Therefore
\begin{align*}
\varepsilon^{-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}K^{\frac32+\eta}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1-2\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta},
\end{align*}
and multiplication by $\varepsilon^{2\alpha}$ proves \eqref{eq:X5-X3-dyadic}.

Finally, choose $\eta<\frac\delta2$. Since $2\alpha-1-\delta-2\delta'>0$, the dyadic characterization of $C_T^{\delta',\,2\alpha-2-\delta-2\delta'}$ gives
\begin{align*}
\left\|\varepsilon^{2\alpha}{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,2\alpha-2-\delta-2\delta'})}\ \lesssim\varepsilon^{2\alpha-1-2\delta'-\eta}\sup_{1\le K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{2\alpha-1-\delta-2\delta'+\eta}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta-2\eta}\longrightarrow0.
\end{align*}
This proves \eqref{eq:X5-X3-vanishing}.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- lemma-16: body_md -->
\label{lem:X5-X2-vanishing}
Let $T>0$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\delta,\delta'>0$ sufficiently small such that $\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'>0$. Then
\begin{align*}
\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha\,{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2}\to0\ \text{in}\ L^p\big(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,\alpha-\frac32-\delta-2\delta'}\big).
\end{align*}
More precisely, if $K:=2^q\vee1$, then, for every sufficiently small $\eta>0$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X2-dyadic}
\left\|\Delta_q\Big(\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha\,{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2}\Big)\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C^{\delta'}([0,T];L^\infty))}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{\alpha-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta}.
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-16 -->
<!-- proof-25: body_md -->
Since $\sup_{\varepsilon\in(0,1]}|\lambda_\varepsilon|<\infty$, we suppress $\lambda_\varepsilon$ throughout the proof. The Wick formula gives
$${\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2}=\sum_{r=0}^2g_r{\mathcal V}_{\varepsilon,r},\ g_r:=\binom5r\binom2r r!,$$
where ${\mathcal V}_{\varepsilon,r}$ is the contribution with exactly $r$ contractions and belongs to the homogeneous Wiener chaos of order $7-2r$.

Repeating the Wiener-isometry estimate from the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:X5-X3-vanishing}, now with $m_2=2-r$, it remains only to estimate the three contraction cases. For $r=0$, the support of $\Phi$ restricts the two resonant frequencies to $|k|\sim|\ell|\sim L$ with $L\gtrsim K$; hence \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-integrated-renorm} with $m=5$, together with $G_{2,\varepsilon}(\ell)\lesssim\varrho(\ell)^{-1}$, leaves only a logarithmic sum over $L$. For $r=1$, we use
\begin{align*}
\int_0^\infty H_{\varepsilon,1}(P,u)\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u\lesssim a_P^{-\frac12},\ \sum_{P\in{\mathbb Z}^3}\varrho(P)^{-2}\varrho(n-P)^{-2}\lesssim\varrho(n)^{-1}.
\end{align*}
The case $r=2$ follows from
$$G_{3,\varepsilon}(n)\lesssim_\eta\Lambda^\eta,\ \int_0^\infty H_{\varepsilon,2}(n,u)\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u
\lesssim\log(2+\Lambda).$$

After absorbing the logarithmic losses, the preceding estimates give
\begin{align*}
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}
{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal V}_{\varepsilon,r}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta
\begin{cases}
\varepsilon^{-\eta},&r=0,2,\\
\varepsilon^{-1-\eta}\varrho(n)^{-1},&r=1.
\end{cases}
\end{align*}
Since the block $|n|\sim K$ contains $O(K^3)$ frequencies and vanishes unless $K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}$, it follows that
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X2-second-moments}
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal V}_{\varepsilon,r}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{-1-\eta}K^{2+\eta},\ r=0,1,2.
\end{align}

For $0\le s<t\le T$, set $h:=t-s$. Choose $\nu>0$ sufficiently small relative to $\eta$ so that $2\delta'+\nu<1$. The time-increment argument leading to \eqref{eq:X5-X3-r012-time-increment} then gives
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X2-time-increments}
\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\big({\mathcal V}_{\varepsilon,r}(t)-{\mathcal V}_{\varepsilon,r}(s)\big)}(n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta h^{2\delta'+\nu}\varepsilon^{-1-4\delta'-\eta}K^{2+\eta},\ r=0,1,2.
\end{align}

The same Bernstein, hypercontractivity, and Banach-space-valued Garsia--Rodemich--Rumsey argument as in the preceding proof yields
\begin{align*}
\|\Delta_q{\mathcal V}_{\varepsilon,r}\|_{L^p(\Omega;C^{\delta'}([0,T];L^\infty))}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta},\ r=0,1,2.
\end{align*}
Multiplying by $\varepsilon^\alpha$, summing over $r$, and using the boundedness of $\lambda_\varepsilon$ proves \eqref{eq:X5-X2-dyadic}.

Finally, choose $0<\eta<\frac\delta2$. Since $\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'>0$, the dyadic characterization gives
\begin{align*}
\left\|\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,\alpha-\frac32-\delta-2\delta'})}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\alpha-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}\sup_{1\le K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'+\eta}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta-2\eta}\to0.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-25 -->
<!-- lemma-17: body_md -->
\label{lem:X5-X1-vanishing}
Let $T>0$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\delta,\delta'>0$ sufficiently small such that $\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'>0$. Then
\begin{align*}
\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha\,{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon\to0\ \text{in}\ L^p\big(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,\alpha-1-\delta-2\delta'}\big).
\end{align*}
More precisely, if $K:=2^q\vee1$, then, for every sufficiently small $\eta>0$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X1-dyadic}
\left\|\Delta_q\Big(\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha\,{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon\Big)\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C^{\delta'}([0,T];L^\infty))}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{\alpha-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}K^{\frac12+\eta}.
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-17 -->
<!-- proof-26: body_md -->
We suppress the uniformly bounded factor $\lambda_\varepsilon$. By the Wick formula,
$${\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon={\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,0}+5{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,1},$$
where ${\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}$ is the contribution with exactly $r$ contractions and belongs to the homogeneous Wiener chaos of order $6-2r$.

For $r=0$, the support of $\Phi$ restricts $\varrho(k)$ and $\varrho(\ell)$ to a common dyadic scale $L\gtrsim K$. The kernel estimate from the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:X5-X3-vanishing}, together with \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-integrated-renorm}, \eqref{eq:X5-X3-basic-convolutions}, and
\begin{align*}
\int_0^\infty H_{\varepsilon,1}(n,u)\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u\lesssim a_n^{-\frac12},
\end{align*}
gives
$$\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}
{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,0}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta\sum_{K\lesssim L\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}K^3L^{-2}\sum_{|k|\sim L}{\mathcal J}_{5,\varepsilon}(k)\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{-\eta}K^2,$$
$$\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,1}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta\sum_{|n|\sim K}G_{4,\varepsilon}(n)a_n^{-1}\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{-1-\eta}K^{1+\eta}.$$
Since the blocks vanish unless $K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}$, these estimates imply
\begin{align*}
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{-1-\eta}K^{1+\eta},\ r=0,1.
\end{align*}

For $0\le s<t\le T$, set $h:=t-s$ and choose $\nu>0$ sufficiently
small relative to $\eta$ so that $2\delta'+\nu<1$. The time-increment
argument leading to \eqref{eq:X5-X3-r012-time-increment} gives
\begin{align}
\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\big({\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}(t)-{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}(s)\big)}(n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta h^{2\delta'+\nu}\varepsilon^{-1-4\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta},\ r=0,1.\nonumber
\end{align}
The same Bernstein, hypercontractivity, and Banach-space-valued Garsia--Rodemich--Rumsey argument as in Lemma~\ref{lem:X5-X3-vanishing} now yields
$$\|\Delta_q{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}\|_{L^p(\Omega;C^{\delta'}([0,T];L^\infty))}\lesssim\varepsilon^{-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}K^{\frac12+\eta},\ r=0,1,$$
Multiplying by $\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha$ and summing over $r$ proves \eqref{eq:X5-X1-dyadic}.

Finally, choose $0<\eta<\frac\delta2$. Since $\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'>0$,
\begin{align*}
\left\|\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,\alpha-1-\delta-2\delta'})}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\alpha-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}\sup_{1\le K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'+\eta}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta-2\eta}\to0.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-26 -->
<!-- lemma-18: body_md -->
\label{lem:mixed-Z2-Z2-vanishing}
Let $T>0$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\delta,\delta'>0$ sufficiently small such that $\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'>0$. Then
\begin{align}\label{eq:mixed-Z2-Z2-vanishing}
\varepsilon^\alpha\,{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\to0,\ \varepsilon^\alpha\,{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2}\to0\ \text{in}\ L^p\big(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,\alpha-1-\delta-2\delta'}\big).
\end{align}
More precisely, if $K:=2^q\vee1$, then, for every sufficiently small $\eta>0$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:Z2-Z2-dyadic-24}
\left\|\Delta_q\Big(\varepsilon^\alpha\,{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\Big)\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C^{\delta'}([0,T];L^\infty))}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{\alpha-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}K^{\frac12+\eta},
\end{align}
\begin{align}\label{eq:Z2-Z2-dyadic-42}
\left\|\Delta_q\Big(\varepsilon^\alpha\,{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2}\Big)\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C^{\delta'}([0,T];L^\infty))}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{\alpha-2\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta}.
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-18 -->
<!-- proof-27: body_md -->
By the Wick formula,
$${\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}=\sum_{r=0}^2c_r{\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(2,4)},\ {\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2}=\sum_{r=0}^2c_r{\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(4,2)},\ c_r:=\binom2r\binom4r r!.$$
Each ${\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(m,n)}$ belongs to the homogeneous Wiener chaos of order $6-2r$. Moreover, all dyadic blocks vanish unless $K\lesssim\Lambda\asymp\varepsilon^{-1}$.

For $m=2,4$, the largest-frequency decomposition used in \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-integrated-renorm} gives
$${\mathcal J}_{m,\varepsilon}(k)\lesssim_\eta\varrho(k)^{-2}\sum_{\substack{N\gtrsim\varrho(k),N\text{ dyadic}}}\frac{N^{m-3+\eta}}{\varrho(k)^2+N^2}\lesssim_\eta\varrho(k)^{m-7+\eta}.$$
Thus,
\begin{align}
{\mathcal J}_{2,\varepsilon}(k)\lesssim_\eta\varrho(k)^{-5+\eta},\ {\mathcal J}_{4,\varepsilon}(k)\lesssim_\eta\varrho(k)^{-3+\eta}.\nonumber
\end{align}

For $|n|\sim K$, the support of $\Phi$ implies
\begin{align*}
\sup_{P\in{\mathbb Z}^3}\sum_{\xi\in{\mathbb Z}^3}|\Phi(P+\xi,n-P-\xi)|\frac{w_\varepsilon(\xi)}{a_{P+\xi}+a_\xi}\lesssim K^{-1}.
\end{align*}
Indeed, the support of $\Phi$ restricts $P+\xi$ and $n-P-\xi$ to comparable frequencies of dyadic size $L\gtrsim K$. For each such $L$, the corresponding sum is bounded by $L^{-1}$: if $\varrho(P)\lesssim L$, this follows from $L^{-2}\sum_{\varrho(\xi)\lesssim L}\varrho(\xi)^{-2}\lesssim L^{-1}$; otherwise, $\varrho(\xi)\asymp\varrho(P)$ and $L^3\varrho(P)^{-4}\lesssim L^{-1}$. Summing over $L$ proves the estimate.

The Wiener-isometry argument from the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:X5-X3-vanishing}, together with the support of $\Phi$, the preceding bounds for ${\mathcal J}_{2,\varepsilon}$ and ${\mathcal J}_{4,\varepsilon}$, and \eqref{eq:X5-X3-basic-convolutions}, gives, uniformly in $t\in[0,T]$ and $|n|\sim K$,
\begin{align*}
{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,0}^{(2,4)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta\Lambda^{1+\eta}K^{-2+\eta},\ {\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,0}^{(4,2)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta K^{-1+\eta}.
\end{align*}
For the contracted terms, the bounds for $H_{\varepsilon,1}$ and $H_{\varepsilon,2}$ from the same proof, the preceding estimate, and
\begin{align*}
\sum_{P\in{\mathbb Z}^3}\varrho(P)^{-2}\varrho(n-P)^{-2}\lesssim\varrho(n)^{-1}
\end{align*}
yield
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,1}^{(2,4)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim K^{-2}G_{4,\varepsilon}(n)\lesssim_\eta\Lambda^{1+\eta}K^{-2},$$
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,2}^{(2,4)}}(t,n)\big|^2
+\sum_{r=1}^2{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(4,2)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta\Lambda^\eta\varrho(n)^{-1}.$$
Since $K\lesssim\Lambda$ and the block contains $O(K^3)$ frequencies, the preceding estimates give, uniformly for $r=0,1,2$,
\begin{align*}
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(2,4)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{-1-\eta}K^{1+\eta},\ \sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(4,2)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{-\eta}K^{2+\eta}.
\end{align*}

For $0\le s<t\le T$, set $h:=t-s$ and choose $\nu>0$ sufficiently small relative to $\eta$ so that $2\delta'+\nu<1$. Repeating the time-increment argument leading to \eqref{eq:X5-X3-r012-time-increment}, we obtain, uniformly for $r=0,1,2$,
$$\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\big({\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(2,4)}(t)-{\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(2,4)}(s)\big)}(n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta h^{2\delta'+\nu}\varepsilon^{-1-4\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta},$$
$$\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\big({\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(4,2)}(t)-{\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(4,2)}(s)\big)}(n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta h^{2\delta'+\nu}\varepsilon^{-4\delta'-\eta}K^{2+\eta}.$$
The same Bernstein, hypercontractivity, and Banach-space-valued Garsia--Rodemich--Rumsey argument as in Lemma~\ref{lem:X5-X3-vanishing} yields, uniformly for $r=0,1,2$,
$$\left\|\Delta_q{\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(2,4)}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C^{\delta'}([0,T];L^\infty))}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}K^{\frac12+\eta},$$
$$\left\|\Delta_q{\mathcal U}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(4,2)}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C^{\delta'}([0,T];L^\infty))}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{-2\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta}.$$
Multiplying by $\varepsilon^\alpha$ and summing over $r$ proves \eqref{eq:Z2-Z2-dyadic-24}--\eqref{eq:Z2-Z2-dyadic-42}.

Finally, choose $0<\eta<\frac\delta2$. Since $\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'>0$, 
$$\left\|\varepsilon^\alpha{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,\alpha-1-\delta-2\delta'})}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\alpha-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}\sup_{1\le K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'+\eta}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta-2\eta},$$
$$\left\|\varepsilon^\alpha{\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond2}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,\alpha-1-\delta-2\delta'})}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\alpha-2\delta'-\eta}\sup_{1\le K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{\alpha-\delta-2\delta'+\eta}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta-2\eta}.$$
Since $\delta-2\eta>0$, this proves \eqref{eq:mixed-Z2-Z2-vanishing}.
<!-- end proof-27 -->
<!-- lemma-19: body_md -->
\label{lem:X3-X3-nonscalar-vanishing}
Let $T>0$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\delta,\delta'>0$ sufficiently small such that $\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'>0$. Then
$$
\varepsilon^\alpha\Big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}-\Pi_0\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}\big)\Big)\to0\ \text{in}\ L^p\big(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\alpha-1-\delta-2\delta'}\big).
$$
More precisely, if $K:=2^q\vee1$, then, for every sufficiently small
$\eta>0$,
$$
\left\|\Delta_q\left[\varepsilon^\alpha\Big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}-\Pi_0\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}\big)\Big)\right]\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta'}L^\infty)}\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{\alpha-2\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta}.
$$
<!-- end lemma-19 -->
<!-- proof-28: body_md -->
By the Wick formula,
$${\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}=\sum_{r=0}^3d_r\widetilde{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r},\ d_r:=\binom{3}{r}^2r!,$$
where $\widetilde{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}$ is the contribution with exactly $r$ contractions and belongs to the homogeneous Wiener chaos of order $6-2r$. Since $d_3\widetilde{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,3}=\Pi_0\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}\big)$, it remains to estimate $r=0,1,2$.

The same Wiener-isometry argument as in Lemma~\ref{lem:X5-X3-vanishing} gives, uniformly in $t\in[0,T]$ and $|n|\sim K$,
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\widetilde{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,0}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\sum_{P+Q=n}|\Phi(P,Q)|^2{\mathcal J}_{3,\varepsilon}(P)G_{3,\varepsilon}(Q),$$
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\widetilde{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,1}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\sum_{P+Q=n}a_P^{-1}G_{2,\varepsilon}(P)G_{2,\varepsilon}(Q),$$
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\widetilde{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,2}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\log^2(2+\Lambda)G_{2,\varepsilon}(n).$$
For $r=0$, the support of $\Phi$ restricts $\varrho(P)$ and $\varrho(Q)$ to comparable dyadic frequencies $L\gtrsim K$. Hence \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-integrated-renorm} with $m=3$ and \eqref{eq:X5-X3-basic-convolutions} give
\begin{align*}
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\widetilde{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,0}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{-\eta}K^3\sum_{L\gtrsim K}L^{-1}\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{-\eta}K^2.
\end{align*}
For $r=1$, the convolution estimate
\begin{align*}
\sum_{P\in{\mathbb Z}^3}\varrho(P)^{-3}\varrho(n-P)^{-1}\lesssim_\eta\varrho(n)^{-1+\eta}
\end{align*}
gives the same bound. The case $r=2$ follows directly from $G_{2,\varepsilon}(n)\lesssim\varrho(n)^{-1}$, with the logarithmic loss absorbed into $\varepsilon^{-\eta}$. Consequently,
\begin{align*}
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\widetilde{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{-\eta}K^{2+\eta},\ r=0,1,2.
\end{align*}

For $0\le s<t\le T$, set $h:=t-s$. Repeating the time-increment argument leading to \eqref{eq:X5-X3-r012-time-increment}, we obtain, for some $\nu>0$ with $2\delta'+\nu<1$ and after absorbing the auxiliary losses into $\eta$,
\begin{align*}
\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\big(\widetilde{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}(t)-\widetilde{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}(s)\big)}(n)\big|^2\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}h^{2\delta'+\nu}\varepsilon^{-4\delta'-\eta}K^{2+\eta},\ r=0,1,2.
\end{align*}
Combining the fixed-time and increment estimates with the Bernstein inequality, hypercontractivity, and the Banach-space-valued Garsia--Rodemich--Rumsey lemma, as in Lemma~\ref{lem:X5-X3-vanishing}, yields
\begin{align*}
\left\|\Delta_q\widetilde{\mathcal T}_{\varepsilon,r}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C^{\delta'}([0,T];L^\infty))}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{-2\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta},\ r=0,1,2.
\end{align*}
Multiplying by $\varepsilon^\alpha$ and summing over $r$ proves the stated dyadic estimate.

Finally, all blocks vanish unless $K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}$. Choosing $0<\eta<\frac\delta2$, the dyadic characterization gives
\begin{align*}
&\left\|\varepsilon^\alpha\Big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}-\Pi_0\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3}\big)\Big)\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,\alpha-1-\delta-2\delta'})}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\varepsilon^{\alpha-2\delta'-\eta}\sup_{1\le K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{\alpha-\delta-2\delta'+\eta}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta-2\eta}\to0.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-28 -->
<!-- lemma-20: body_md -->
\label{lem:X4-X4-nonscalar-vanishing}
Let $T>0$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\delta,\delta'>0$ sufficiently small such that $\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'>0$. Then
$$
\varepsilon^{2\alpha}\Big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}-\Pi_0\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)\Big)\to0\ \text{in}\ L^p\big(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',2\alpha-2-\delta-2\delta'}\big).
$$
More precisely, if $K:=2^q\vee1$, then, for every sufficiently small $\eta>0$,
\begin{align*}
\left\|\Delta_q\left[\varepsilon^{2\alpha}\Big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}-\Pi_0\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)\Big)\right]\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C^{\delta'}([0,T];L^\infty))}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{2\alpha-1-2\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta}.\end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-20 -->
<!-- proof-29: body_md -->
By the Wick formula,
$${\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}=\sum_{r=0}^4e_r{\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,r},\ e_r:=\binom4r^2r!,$$
where ${\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,r}$ is the contribution with exactly $r$ contractions and belongs to the homogeneous Wiener chaos of order $8-2r$. Since $e_4{\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,4}=\Pi_0\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)$, it remains to estimate $r=0,1,2,3$.

The same Wiener-isometry argument as in Lemma~\ref{lem:X5-X3-vanishing} gives, uniformly in $t\in[0,T]$ and $|n|\sim K$,
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,0}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\sum_{P+Q=n}|\Phi(P,Q)|^2{\mathcal J}_{4,\varepsilon}(P)G_{4,\varepsilon}(Q),$$
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,1}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\sum_{P+Q=n}a_P^{-1}G_{3,\varepsilon}(P)G_{3,\varepsilon}(Q),$$
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,2}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\log^2(2+\Lambda)G_{4,\varepsilon}(n).$$
For $r=0$, the support of $\Phi$ restricts $\varrho(P)$ and $\varrho(Q)$ to comparable dyadic frequencies $L\gtrsim K$, so we use \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-integrated-renorm} with $m=4$. For $r=1$, we use $G_{3,\varepsilon}\lesssim_\eta\Lambda^\eta$ and $\sum_{\varrho(P)\lesssim\Lambda}a_P^{-1}\lesssim\Lambda$, while the case $r=2$ follows from $G_{4,\varepsilon}\lesssim_\eta\Lambda^{1+\eta}$. After absorbing the logarithmic summations and convolution losses into $\eta$, we obtain
$$\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,r}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{-1-\eta}K^3,\ r=0,1,2.$$

For $r=3$, the estimate
$$H_{\varepsilon,3}(P,u)\lesssim\mathrm{e}^{-ca_Pu}\big(\Lambda^3\wedge u^{-\frac32}\big)$$
implies
$$\int_0^\infty H_{\varepsilon,3}(P,u)\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u\lesssim\Lambda.$$
Consequently,
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,3}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\Lambda^2G_{2,\varepsilon}(n),$$
and hence
$$\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,3}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\varepsilon^{-2}K^2.$$
Since all blocks vanish unless $K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}$, the estimates for $r=0,1,2$ are also bounded by $\varepsilon^{-2-\eta}K^2$. Therefore,
$$\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,r}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{-2-\eta}K^2,\ r=0,1,2,3.$$

Let $h:=t-s>0$. Using \eqref{eq:X5-X3-first-chaos-increment} and repeating the time-increment argument from the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:X5-X3-vanishing}, we obtain, for some sufficiently small $\nu>0$ satisfying $2\delta'+\nu<1$,
$$\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\big({\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,r}(t)-{\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,r}(s)\big)}(n)\big|^2\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}h^{2\delta'+\nu}\varepsilon^{-2-4\delta'-\eta}K^2,\ r=0,1,2,3.$$
Combining the fixed-time and increment estimates with hypercontractivity, the Bernstein inequality, and the Banach-space-valued Garsia--Rodemich--Rumsey lemma yields
$$\left\|\Delta_q{\mathcal S}_{\varepsilon,r}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta'}L^\infty)}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{-1-2\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta},\ r=0,1,2,3.$$
Multiplying by $\varepsilon^{2\alpha}$ and summing over $r$ proves the stated dyadic estimate.

Finally, choose $0<\eta<\frac\delta2$. Since all blocks vanish unless $K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}$ and $2\alpha-1-\delta-2\delta'+\eta>0$, 
\begin{align*}
&\left\|\varepsilon^{2\alpha}\Big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}-\Pi_0\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)\Big)\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,2\alpha-2-\delta-2\delta'})}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\lesssim\varepsilon^{2\alpha-1-2\delta'-\eta}\sup_{1\le K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{2\alpha-1-\delta-2\delta'+\eta}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta-2\eta}\to0.
\end{align*}
<!-- end proof-29 -->
<!-- lemma-21: body_md -->
\label{lem:X3-X4-D-localization}
Let $T>0$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\delta,\delta'>0$ sufficiently small such that $\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'>0$. Then
\begin{align*}
\frac53\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha\,\Pi_1\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)-2D_{\varepsilon,\alpha}X_\varepsilon\to0\ \hbox{in }L^p\big(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\alpha-\frac32-\delta-2\delta'}\big),
\end{align*}
and
\begin{align*}
\frac53\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha\,(1-\Pi_1)\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)\to0\ \hbox{in }L^p\big(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\alpha-\frac32-\delta-2\delta'}\big).
\end{align*}
More precisely, if $K:=2^q\vee1$, then, for every sufficiently small $\eta>0$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:X3-X4-D-first-chaos-dyadic}
\left\|\Delta_q\left(\frac53\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha\,\Pi_1\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)-2D_{\varepsilon,\alpha}X_\varepsilon\right)\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta'}L^\infty)}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{\alpha-2\delta'-\eta}K^{\frac32+\eta},
\end{align}
\begin{align}\label{eq:X3-X4-D-higher-chaos-dyadic}
\left\|\Delta_q\left(\frac53\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha(1-\Pi_1)\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)\right)\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta'}L^\infty)}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{\alpha-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta}.
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-21 -->
<!-- proof-30: body_md -->
Since $(\lambda_\varepsilon)_{\varepsilon\in(0,1]}$ is bounded, we suppress this factor in the estimates below. By the Wick formula,
$${\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}=\sum_{r=0}^3h_r{\mathcal W}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(3,4)},\ h_r:=\binom3r\binom4r r!,$$
where ${\mathcal W}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(3,4)}$ belongs to the homogeneous Wiener chaos of order $7-2r$. The summand corresponding to $r=3$ is the first-chaos projection.

For $n,k\in{\mathbb Z}^3$, set
$$\Phi_n(k):=\Phi(k,n-k),\ \Gamma_{\varepsilon,3}(n):=\sum_{k\in{\mathbb Z}^3}\Phi_n(k)a_k{\mathcal J}_{3,\varepsilon}(k).$$
Then
$$\Phi_0(k)=1,\ |\Phi_n(k)-1|\lesssim{\boldsymbol{1}}_{\{\varrho(k)\lesssim\varrho(n)\}}+\frac{\varrho(n)}{\varrho(k)}{\boldsymbol{1}}_{\{\varrho(k)\gg\varrho(n)\}}.$$
Moreover, the largest-frequency decomposition used in the proof of \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-integrated-renorm} gives
$$\sum_{\varrho(k)\sim L}a_k{\mathcal J}_{3,\varepsilon}(k)\lesssim L,\ 1\le L\lesssim\Lambda.$$
Decomposing the sum at $L\sim\varrho(n)$ therefore yields, for every $\kappa>0$,
\begin{align*}
|\Gamma_{\varepsilon,3}(n)-\Gamma_{\varepsilon,3}(0)|\lesssim\sum_{L\lesssim\varrho(n)}L+\sum_{\varrho(n)\ll L\lesssim\Lambda}\varrho(n)\lesssim\varrho(n)\log(2+\Lambda)\lesssim_\kappa\varepsilon^{-\kappa}\varrho(n).
\end{align*}

A direct Fourier computation gives
$$\widehat{\Pi_1\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)}(t,n)=24\Gamma_{\varepsilon,3}(n)\widehat X_\varepsilon(t,n).$$
Moreover, \eqref{eq:D-def} and $\Phi_0(k)=1$ give
$$D_{\varepsilon,\alpha}=\frac{10}{3}\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha\,3!\,\Gamma_{\varepsilon,3}(0).$$
Thus, setting
$$F_\varepsilon^{(1)}:=\frac53\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha\Pi_1\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)-2D_{\varepsilon,\alpha}X_\varepsilon,$$
we obtain
$$\widehat F_\varepsilon^{(1)}(t,n)=40\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha\big(\Gamma_{\varepsilon,3}(n)-\Gamma_{\varepsilon,3}(0)\big)\widehat X_\varepsilon(t,n).$$

Let $h:=t-s>0$ and choose $\nu>0$ such that $\gamma:=2\delta'+\nu<1$. Using
\eqref{eq:X5-X3-first-chaos-increment} and $\sum_{|n|\sim K}w_\varepsilon(n)\lesssim K$, we obtain
$$\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_qF_\varepsilon^{(1)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\kappa\varepsilon^{2\alpha-\kappa}K^3,$$
$$\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\big(F_\varepsilon^{(1)}(t)-F_\varepsilon^{(1)}(s)\big)}(n)\big|^2\lesssim_\kappa h^\gamma\varepsilon^{2\alpha-2\gamma-\kappa}K^3.$$
Hypercontractivity, the Bernstein inequality, and the Banach-space-valued Garsia--Rodemich--Rumsey lemma then yield
$$\left\|\Delta_qF_\varepsilon^{(1)}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta'}L^\infty)}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{\alpha-2\delta'-\eta}K^{\frac32+\eta}.$$
This proves \eqref{eq:X3-X4-D-first-chaos-dyadic}.

It remains to estimate $r=0,1,2$. The same Wiener-isometry argument as in Lemma~\ref{lem:X5-X3-vanishing} gives, uniformly in $t\in[0,T]$ and $|n|\sim K$,
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal W}_{\varepsilon,0}^{(3,4)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\sum_{P+Q=n}|\Phi(P,Q)|^2{\mathcal J}_{3,\varepsilon}(P)G_{4,\varepsilon}(Q),$$
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal W}_{\varepsilon,1}^{(3,4)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\sum_{P+Q=n}a_P^{-1}G_{2,\varepsilon}(P)G_{3,\varepsilon}(Q),$$
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal W}_{\varepsilon,2}^{(3,4)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\log^2(2+\Lambda)G_{3,\varepsilon}(n).$$
For $r=0$, the support of $\Phi$ restricts $\varrho(P)$ and $\varrho(Q)$ to comparable dyadic frequencies $L\gtrsim K$. Using \eqref{eq:dyadic-conv-integrated-renorm} with $m=3$ and $G_{4,\varepsilon}\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{-1-\eta}$ gives a bound of order $\varepsilon^{-1-\eta}K^2$. For $r=1$, we use
$$G_{3,\varepsilon}\lesssim_\eta\Lambda^\eta,\ \sum_{\varrho(P)\lesssim\Lambda}a_P^{-1}G_{2,\varepsilon}(P)\lesssim\log(2+\Lambda),$$
while the case $r=2$ follows directly from the bound on $G_{3,\varepsilon}$. Since $K\lesssim\Lambda\asymp\varepsilon^{-1}$, the last two bounds are also dominated by $\varepsilon^{-1-\eta}K^2$. Hence
$$\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal W}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(3,4)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\eta\varepsilon^{-1-\eta}K^{2+\eta},\ r=0,1,2.$$

For $0\le s<t\le T$, set $h:=t-s$. Repeating the time-increment argument leading to \eqref{eq:X5-X3-r012-time-increment}, we obtain, for some $\nu>0$ with $2\delta'+\nu<1$,
$$\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\big({\mathcal W}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(3,4)}(t)-{\mathcal W}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(3,4)}(s)\big)}(n)\big|^2\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}h^{2\delta'+\nu}\varepsilon^{-1-4\delta'-\eta}K^{2+\eta},\ r=0,1,2.$$
Together with the fixed-time estimate, hypercontractivity, the Bernstein inequality, and the Banach-space-valued Garsia--Rodemich--Rumsey lemma, this gives
$$\left\|\Delta_q{\mathcal W}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(3,4)}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta'}L^\infty)}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}K^{1+\eta},\ r=0,1,2.$$
Since
$$(1-\Pi_1)\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)=\sum_{r=0}^2h_r{\mathcal W}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(3,4)},$$
the boundedness of $\lambda_\varepsilon$ proves \eqref{eq:X3-X4-D-higher-chaos-dyadic}.

Finally, choose $0<\eta<\frac\delta2$. Since all blocks vanish unless $K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}$ and the relevant powers of $K$ are positive, the two dyadic estimates give
$$
\left\|F_\varepsilon^{(1)}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,\alpha-\frac32-\delta-2\delta'})}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\alpha-2\delta'-\eta}\sup_{1\le K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{\alpha-\delta-2\delta'+\eta}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta-2\eta},$$
$$\left\|\frac53\lambda_\varepsilon\varepsilon^\alpha(1-\Pi_1)\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond3})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,\alpha-\frac32-\delta-2\delta'})}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\alpha-\frac12-2\delta'-\eta}\sup_{1\le K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'+\eta}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta-2\eta}.$$
Both terms therefore converge to zero.
<!-- end proof-30 -->
<!-- lemma-22: body_md -->
\label{lem:X5-X4-B-localization}
Let $T>0$, $p\in[1,\infty)$, $\delta,\delta'>0$ sufficiently small such that $\alpha-\frac12-\delta-2\delta'>0$. Then
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X4-B-first-chaos}
\frac53\varepsilon^{2\alpha}\,\Pi_1\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)-3B_{\varepsilon,\alpha}X_\varepsilon\to0\ \text{in }L^p\big(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,2\alpha-\frac52-\delta-2\delta'}\big),
\end{align}
and
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X4-B-higher-chaos}
\frac53\varepsilon^{2\alpha}\,(1-\Pi_1)\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)\to0\ \text{in }L^p\big(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,2\alpha-\frac52-\delta-2\delta'}\big).
\end{align}
More precisely, if $K:=2^q\vee1$, then, for every sufficiently small $\eta>0$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X4-B-first-chaos-dyadic}
\left\|\Delta_q\left(\frac53\varepsilon^{2\alpha}\,\Pi_1\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)-3B_{\varepsilon,\alpha}X_\varepsilon\right)\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta'}L^\infty)}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{2\alpha-1-2\delta'-\eta}K^{\frac32+\eta},
\end{align}
and
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X4-B-higher-chaos-dyadic}
\left\|\Delta_q\left(\frac53\varepsilon^{2\alpha}(1-\Pi_1)\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)\right)\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta'}L^\infty)}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{2\alpha-1-2\delta'-\eta}K^{\frac32+\eta}.
\end{align}
<!-- end lemma-22 -->
<!-- proof-31: body_md -->
For $\mathbf k=(k_1,\ldots,k_4)\in({\mathbb Z}^3)^4$, write
$$k:=k_1+\cdots+k_4,\ W_\varepsilon(\mathbf k):=\prod_{i=1}^4w_\varepsilon(k_i),\ R_n(\mathbf k):=a_{k+n}+\sum_{i=1}^4a_{k_i},$$
and set
$$\Psi(k,n):=\sum_{|i-j|\le1}\varphi_i(k+n)\varphi_j(-k).$$
Note that $\Psi(k,0)=1$. The term with four cross-contractions is
$$\widehat{\Pi_1\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)}(t,n)=120\sum_{\mathbf k}\Psi(k,n)W_\varepsilon(\mathbf k)\int_0^\infty\mathrm{e}^{-R_n(\mathbf k)u}\widehat X_\varepsilon(t-u,n)\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u.$$
Set
$$F_\varepsilon^{(1)}:=\frac53\varepsilon^{2\alpha}\Pi_1\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)-3B_{\varepsilon,\alpha}X_\varepsilon.
$$
Equation \eqref{eq:B5-explicit} gives
\begin{align*}
\frac{\widehat{F_\varepsilon^{(1)}}(t,n)}{200\varepsilon^{2\alpha}}={}&\sum_{\mathbf k}W_\varepsilon(\mathbf k)\left(\frac{\Psi(k,n)}{R_n(\mathbf k)}-\frac1{R_0(\mathbf k)}\right)\widehat X_\varepsilon(t,n)\\
&+\sum_{\mathbf k}\Psi(k,n)W_\varepsilon(\mathbf k)\int_0^\infty\mathrm{e}^{-R_n(\mathbf k)u}\big(\widehat X_\varepsilon(t-u,n)-\widehat X_\varepsilon(t,n)\big)\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u.
\end{align*}
The first term measures the change in the resonant multiplier and heat denominator when $n$ is set to zero; the second is the error from replacing $\widehat X_\varepsilon(t-u,n)$ by $\widehat X_\varepsilon(t,n)$.

We first estimate the coefficient in the first term. For every $\kappa>0$,
\begin{align}\label{eq:X5-X4-localization-sum}
\sum_{\varrho(k)\sim L}\sum_{k_1+\cdots+k_4=k}\frac{W_\varepsilon(\mathbf k)}{R_n(\mathbf k)}\lesssim_\kappa L^{2+\kappa},\ 1\le L\lesssim\Lambda,
\end{align}
uniformly in $n$. Indeed, for fixed $k$, decompose the sum according to $N:={\mathord{{\rm max}}}_i\varrho(k_i)\gtrsim L$. The same largest-frequency argument as in \eqref{eq:X5-X3-basic-convolutions} bounds the corresponding convolution sum by $N^{1+\kappa}$, whereas $R_n(\mathbf k)\gtrsim N^2$. Summing over dyadic $N\gtrsim L$ and over the $O(L^3)$ possible values of $k$ proves \eqref{eq:X5-X4-localization-sum}.

Moreover,
$$|\Psi(k,n)-1|\lesssim{\boldsymbol{1}}_{\{\varrho(k)\lesssim\varrho(n)\}}+\frac{\varrho(n)}{\varrho(k)}{\boldsymbol{1}}_{\{\varrho(k)\gg\varrho(n)\}},\ |a_{k+n}-a_k|\lesssim\varrho(n)\big(\varrho(k)+\varrho(n)\big).$$
Splitting at $\varrho(k)\sim\varrho(n)$ and using $R_n(\mathbf k)\asymp R_0(\mathbf k)$ when $\varrho(k)\gg\varrho(n)$ gives, for the only relevant range $\varrho(n)\lesssim\Lambda$,
$$\sum_{\mathbf k}W_\varepsilon(\mathbf k)\Big|\frac{\Psi(k,n)}{R_n(\mathbf k)}-\frac1{R_0(\mathbf k)}\Big|\lesssim_\kappa\Lambda^{1+\kappa}\varrho(n).$$

Choose $\gamma>2\delta'$ sufficiently close to $2\delta'$ and then $\kappa>0$ sufficiently small that $\gamma+\kappa<1$. Writing $h:=|t-s|$, we have
$${\mathbb E}\left|\widehat X_\varepsilon(t,n)-\widehat X_\varepsilon(s,n)\right|^2\lesssim h^\gamma a_n^\gamma w_\varepsilon(n),\ \sum_{|n|\sim K}w_\varepsilon(n)\lesssim K.$$
This controls the first term in the decomposition of $F_\varepsilon^{(1)}$.

For the second term, set $\rho:=\frac{1-\gamma-\kappa}2$. Since $R_n(\mathbf k)\gtrsim{\mathord{{\rm max}}}_i\varrho(k_i)^2$ and $\sum_{\varrho(k)\lesssim N}w_\varepsilon(k)\lesssim N$,
$$\sum_{\mathbf k}W_\varepsilon(\mathbf k)R_n(\mathbf k)^{-1-\rho}\lesssim\sum_{\substack{N\lesssim\Lambda\\N\text{ dyadic}}}N^{2-2\rho}\lesssim\Lambda^{2-2\rho}.$$
The covariance formula for the Ornstein--Uhlenbeck modes gives
$$\left\|\widehat X_\varepsilon(t-u,n)-\widehat X_\varepsilon(t,n)\right\|_{L^2(\Omega)}\lesssim u^\rho a_n^\rho w_\varepsilon(n)^{\frac12},$$
$$\left\|\big(\widehat X_\varepsilon(t-u,n)-\widehat X_\varepsilon(t,n)\big)-\big(\widehat X_\varepsilon(s-u,n)-\widehat X_\varepsilon(s,n)\big)\right\|_{L^2(\Omega)}\lesssim h^{\frac\gamma2}u^\rho a_n^{\frac\gamma2+\rho}w_\varepsilon(n)^{\frac12}.$$
Using the Minkowski inequality and $K\lesssim\Lambda$, the two terms in
the decomposition of $F_\varepsilon^{(1)}$ therefore satisfy the common bounds
$$\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_qF_\varepsilon^{(1)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\kappa\varepsilon^{4\alpha}\Lambda^{2+2\gamma+2\kappa}K^3,$$
$$\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\big(F_\varepsilon^{(1)}(t)-F_\varepsilon^{(1)}(s)\big)}(n)
\big|^2\lesssim_\kappa h^\gamma\varepsilon^{4\alpha}\Lambda^{2+2\gamma+2\kappa}K^3.
$$
Hypercontractivity, the Bernstein inequality, and the Banach-space-valued Garsia--Rodemich--Rumsey lemma now yield, after absorbing the auxiliary losses into $\eta$,
$$\left\|\Delta_qF_\varepsilon^{(1)}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta'}L^\infty)}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{2\alpha-1-2\delta'-\eta}K^{\frac32+\eta}.$$
This proves \eqref{eq:X5-X4-B-first-chaos-dyadic}.

It remains to control the higher-chaos part. By the Wick formula,
$$(1-\Pi_1)\big({\mathcal I}(X_\varepsilon^{\diamond5})\circ X_\varepsilon^{\diamond4}\big)=\sum_{r=0}^3\binom5r\binom4r r!\,{\mathcal Q}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(5,4)},$$
where ${\mathcal Q}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(5,4)}$ is the contribution with exactly $r$ cross-contractions and belongs to the homogeneous Wiener chaos of order $9-2r$.

Besides \eqref{eq:X5-X3-basic-convolutions}, we only need
$$G_{5,\varepsilon}(b)\lesssim_\kappa\Lambda^{2+\kappa},\ \int_0^\infty H_{\varepsilon,3}(P,u)\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u\lesssim\int_0^\infty\mathrm{e}^{-ca_Pu}\big(\Lambda^3\wedge u^{-\frac32}\big)\,{\mathord{{\rm d}}} u\lesssim\Lambda.$$
The same Wiener-isometry estimates as in Lemma~\ref{lem:X5-X3-vanishing} give, uniformly in $t\in[0,T]$ and $|n|\sim K$,
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal Q}_{\varepsilon,0}^{(5,4)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\sum_{P+Q=n}|\Phi(P,Q)|^2{\mathcal J}_{5,\varepsilon}(P)G_{4,\varepsilon}(Q),$$
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal Q}_{\varepsilon,1}^{(5,4)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\sum_{P+Q=n}a_P^{-1}G_{4,\varepsilon}(P)G_{3,\varepsilon}(Q),$$
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal Q}_{\varepsilon,2}^{(5,4)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\log^2(2+\Lambda)G_{5,\varepsilon}(n),$$
$${\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal Q}_{\varepsilon,3}^{(5,4)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim\Lambda^2G_{3,\varepsilon}(n).$$
Together with \eqref{eq:X5-X3-integrated-five-sum}, \eqref{eq:X5-X3-basic-convolutions}, and $\sum_{\varrho(P)\lesssim\Lambda}a_P^{-1}\lesssim\Lambda$, this yields
$$\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q{\mathcal Q}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(5,4)}}(t,n)\big|^2\lesssim_\kappa\Lambda^{2+\kappa}K^3,\ r=0,1,2,3.$$
Using \eqref{eq:X5-X3-first-chaos-increment} and the time-increment argument from Lemma~\ref{lem:X5-X3-vanishing}, we similarly obtain
$$\sum_{|n|\sim K}{\mathbb E}\big|\widehat{\Delta_q\big({\mathcal Q}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(5,4)}(t)-{\mathcal Q}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(5,4)}(s)\big)}(n)\big|^2\lesssim_\kappa h^\gamma\Lambda^{2+2\gamma+\kappa}K^3,\ r=0,1,2,3.$$
Consequently,
$$\left\|\Delta_q{\mathcal Q}_{\varepsilon,r}^{(5,4)}\right\|_{L^p(\Omega;C_T^{\delta'}L^\infty)}\lesssim_{\eta,\delta'}\varepsilon^{-1-2\delta'-\eta}K^{\frac32+\eta},\ r=0,1,2,3.$$
Multiplying by $\frac53\varepsilon^{2\alpha}$ and summing over $r$ proves
\eqref{eq:X5-X4-B-higher-chaos-dyadic}.

Finally, choose $0<\eta<\frac\delta2$. Both expressions in \eqref{eq:X5-X4-B-first-chaos} and \eqref{eq:X5-X4-B-higher-chaos} have no dyadic blocks above $K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}$ and satisfy the same dyadic estimate. Since $2\alpha-1-\delta-2\delta'+\eta>0$, their norms in  $L^p\big(\Omega;C_T^{\delta',\,2\alpha-\frac52-\delta-2\delta'}\big)$ are bounded by
$$\varepsilon^{2\alpha-1-2\delta'-\eta}\sup_{1\le K\lesssim\varepsilon^{-1}}K^{2\alpha-1-\delta-2\delta'+\eta}\lesssim\varepsilon^{\delta-2\eta}\to0.$$
This proves both convergence statements.
<!-- end proof-31 -->
